# English Lower Sixth, Lower Sixth English: authored answers for review

Every answer fixed by a person, not computed. Part 1: the tables, conventions and rules that computed answers come from.
Part 2: every authored question template, with all its data (keys, statements, pairs, choices and feedback) and sources.
Regenerate with `npm run review:l6-english`. Item IDs (TR-…) match `docs/teacher-review.md`.

## Part 1

Rules the computed answers use are listed in the subject’s library tests; every authored answer is in Part 2.

### `el6-2-terms` · Lesson 2 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means the subject discussed without the full controlling claim? | topic | main idea · supporting detail · scope limit | That concerns the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim. · That concerns information that develops or illustrates the controlling point, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim. · That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim. | A main idea says something about the topic. |
| Which term means the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details? | main idea | topic · supporting detail · scope limit | That concerns the subject discussed without the full controlling claim, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details. · That concerns information that develops or illustrates the controlling point, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details. · That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details. | The trial’s context and limited evidence must be preserved. |
| Which term means information that develops or illustrates the controlling point? | supporting detail | topic · main idea · scope limit | That concerns the subject discussed without the full controlling claim, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point. · That concerns the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point. · That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point. | A detail supports a broader point rather than necessarily replacing it. |
| Which term means a boundary on what the passage establishes? | scope limit | topic · main idea · supporting detail | That concerns the subject discussed without the full controlling claim, rather than a boundary on what the passage establishes. · That concerns the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details, rather than a boundary on what the passage establishes. · That concerns information that develops or illustrates the controlling point, rather than a boundary on what the passage establishes. | The passage explicitly limits the comparison. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Which term means information that develops or illustrates the controlling point?

- ✅ supporting detail
- ◻️ scope limit  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point._
- ◻️ topic  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns the subject discussed without the full controlling claim, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point._
- ◻️ main idea  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details, rather than information that develops or illustrates the controlling point._

Full working after a second miss:

> A detail supports a broader point rather than necessarily replacing it.  
> So the answer is “supporting detail”.  

> Which term means the subject discussed without the full controlling claim?

- ✅ topic
- ◻️ main idea  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim._
- ◻️ scope limit  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim._
- ◻️ supporting detail  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns information that develops or illustrates the controlling point, rather than the subject discussed without the full controlling claim._

Full working after a second miss:

> A main idea says something about the topic.  
> So the answer is “topic”.  

> Which term means the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details?

- ◻️ supporting detail  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns information that develops or illustrates the controlling point, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details._
- ◻️ topic  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns the subject discussed without the full controlling claim, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details._
- ◻️ scope limit  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a boundary on what the passage establishes, rather than the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details._
- ✅ main idea

Full working after a second miss:

> The trial’s context and limited evidence must be preserved.  
> So the answer is “main idea”.

### `el6-2-apply` · Lesson 2 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original passage: Bih’s club tested shaded seating. Members used it at midday, but rainy-day use was not recorded. Which main idea preserves scope? | A seating trial attracted midday use, with evidence still limited | Rain never affects seating · Every school must build the same seats · Bih owns all the chairs | Rainy-day evidence is missing. · The trial does not establish a universal requirement. · Ownership is not stated. | Cover both the observed use and the evidence limit. |
| Original passage: A fictional market team labelled separate collection bins and explained the labels. Sorting improved during observed mornings; afternoon use remains unknown. Which is the main idea? | A labelling-and-explanation trial improved observed sorting, with incomplete coverage | The only topic is the colour of paper · All waste problems ended permanently · Afternoons were proved identical | The passage concerns a sorting intervention. · Only a limited observation is reported. · Afternoon use was not observed. | A main idea covers intervention, result and limitation. |
| Original passage: Ayuk’s group shared a timetable to avoid room clashes. Reported clashes fell, though the group had no record of informal meetings. Which statement fits the whole passage? | A shared timetable reduced reported clashes but the record was incomplete | Informal meetings never happen · The timetable is irrelevant · No meeting ever clashed again | Missing records do not prove absence. · The reported change concerns the timetable. · The claim exceeds the reported evidence. | Keep the central change without turning incomplete records into certainty. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Original passage: Ayuk’s group shared a timetable to avoid room clashes. Reported clashes fell, though the group had no record of informal meetings. Which statement fits the whole passage?

- ◻️ Informal meetings never happen  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Missing records do not prove absence._
- ◻️ The timetable is irrelevant  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The reported change concerns the timetable._
- ✅ A shared timetable reduced reported clashes but the record was incomplete
- ◻️ No meeting ever clashed again  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The claim exceeds the reported evidence._

Full working after a second miss:

> Keep the central change without turning incomplete records into certainty.  
> So the answer is “A shared timetable reduced reported clashes but the record was incomplete”.  

> Original passage: A fictional market team labelled separate collection bins and explained the labels. Sorting improved during observed mornings; afternoon use remains unknown. Which is the main idea?

- ◻️ All waste problems ended permanently  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Only a limited observation is reported._
- ✅ A labelling-and-explanation trial improved observed sorting, with incomplete coverage
- ◻️ Afternoons were proved identical  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Afternoon use was not observed._
- ◻️ The only topic is the colour of paper  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The passage concerns a sorting intervention._

Full working after a second miss:

> A main idea covers intervention, result and limitation.  
> So the answer is “A labelling-and-explanation trial improved observed sorting, with incomplete coverage”.  

> Original passage: Ayuk’s group shared a timetable to avoid room clashes. Reported clashes fell, though the group had no record of informal meetings. Which statement fits the whole passage?

- ◻️ No meeting ever clashed again  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The claim exceeds the reported evidence._
- ✅ A shared timetable reduced reported clashes but the record was incomplete
- ◻️ Informal meetings never happen  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Missing records do not prove absence._
- ◻️ The timetable is irrelevant

### `el6-2-repair` · Lesson 2 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ topic: the subject discussed without the full controlling claim.
- ✅ main idea: the controlling point that accounts for the passage’s relevant details.
- ✅ supporting detail: information that develops or illustrates the controlling point.
- ✅ scope limit: a boundary on what the passage establishes.
- ❌ A topic label alone expresses every argument.  
  _↳ A main idea says something about the topic._
- ❌ The passage proves every library needs identical slips.  
  _↳ The trial’s context and limited evidence must be preserved._

### `el6-3-terms` · Lesson 3 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means the people a text principally addresses? | audience | purpose · tone · language evidence | That concerns the result the writer seeks through the text, rather than the people a text principally addresses. · That concerns the attitude conveyed by language choices, rather than the people a text principally addresses. · That concerns a particular phrase supporting an interpretation, rather than the people a text principally addresses. | References and required actions identify likely readers. |
| Which term means the result the writer seeks through the text? | purpose | audience · tone · language evidence | That concerns the people a text principally addresses, rather than the result the writer seeks through the text. · That concerns the attitude conveyed by language choices, rather than the result the writer seeks through the text. · That concerns a particular phrase supporting an interpretation, rather than the result the writer seeks through the text. | Intended effect differs from observed response. |
| Which term means the attitude conveyed by language choices? | tone | audience · purpose · language evidence | That concerns the people a text principally addresses, rather than the attitude conveyed by language choices. · That concerns the result the writer seeks through the text, rather than the attitude conveyed by language choices. · That concerns a particular phrase supporting an interpretation, rather than the attitude conveyed by language choices. | Tone and factual reliability are different judgements. |
| Which term means a particular phrase supporting an interpretation? | language evidence | audience · purpose · tone | That concerns the people a text principally addresses, rather than a particular phrase supporting an interpretation. · That concerns the result the writer seeks through the text, rather than a particular phrase supporting an interpretation. · That concerns the attitude conveyed by language choices, rather than a particular phrase supporting an interpretation. | Interpretation must be supported by textual choices. |

- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”

- ◻️ language evidence  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a particular phrase supporting an interpretation, rather than the attitude conveyed by language choices._

### `el6-3-apply` · Lesson 3 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original line to club members: Please suggest a suitable meeting time and explain your choice. Which purpose is best supported? | Requesting suggestions with reasons | Announcing a meeting already completed · Insulting every member · Giving a final exam result | The line asks for future input. · Its polite request contains no insult. · No result is stated. | Please suggest and explain identify the intended action. |
| Original appeal to readers: Support the reading corner; your donated books could widen its collection. Which interpretation is supported? | Persuading readers to contribute books | Proving every reader already donated · Ordering a court judgement · Describing only a past holiday | An appeal is not evidence of compliance. · No legal judgement is involved. · The wording seeks a contribution. | The request and projected benefit support a persuasive purpose. |
| Original message to a friend: I was relieved when your letter arrived. Which tone is supported by relieved? | Personal relief | Unqualified hostility · A neutral list of measurements · Proof that all earlier worries were imaginary | Relieved conveys a different attitude. · The word expresses a personal feeling. · A feeling does not establish that previous concerns lacked basis. | Tie tone to the actual word without inventing background facts. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

- ◻️ Unqualified hostility  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Relieved conveys a different attitude._
- ◻️ A neutral list of measurements  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The word expresses a personal feeling._

Full working after a second miss:

> Tie tone to the actual word without inventing background facts.

> Original message to a friend: I was relieved when your letter arrived. Which tone is supported by relieved?

- ✅ Personal relief
- ◻️ A neutral list of measurements  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The word expresses a personal feeling._
- ◻️ Proof that all earlier worries were imaginary  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: A feeling does not establish that previous concerns lacked basis._
- ◻️ Unqualified hostility  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Relieved conveys a different attitude._

Full working after a second miss:

> So the answer is “Personal relief”.

- ◻️ Insulting every member

- ◻️ Announcing a meeting already completed

### `el6-4-apply` · Lesson 4 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original excerpt: A request enters the register, the organiser checks availability, and a reply confirms the booking. Its main function is to explain the process. Which category fits? | Expository | Argumentative solely because it has verbs · Descriptive solely because it names an organiser · A poem proved by the commas | Verbs occur in many text types. · Naming a person does not establish description as dominant. · Punctuation does not prove verse. | The stated dominant function is process explanation. |
| Original excerpt: The hall should stay open later because many pupils arrive after chores. Which dominant function is supported? | Argumentative | Only a neutral physical description · Only a list with no claim · A narrative solely because pupils are mentioned | It advocates a policy with a reason. · Should and because connect claim and reason. · Mentioning people does not establish an event sequence. | Identify the proposal and its support. |
| Original excerpt: The pale walls enclosed a cool, echoing room. The dominant function is what? | Descriptive | An explicit policy argument · A complete sequence of several events · A set of transport calculations | No policy claim is advanced. · The line presents qualities rather than a sequence. · No quantities or calculations appear. | Selected sensory qualities build an impression. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Original excerpt: A request enters the register, the organiser checks availability, and a reply confirms the booking. Its main function is to explain the process. Which category fits?

- ◻️ Descriptive solely because it names an organiser  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Naming a person does not establish description as dominant._
- ✅ Expository
- ◻️ Argumentative solely because it has verbs  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Verbs occur in many text types._
- ◻️ A poem proved by the commas  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Punctuation does not prove verse._

Full working after a second miss:

> Original excerpt: The pale walls enclosed a cool, echoing room. The dominant function is what?

- ◻️ A complete sequence of several events  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The line presents qualities rather than a sequence._
- ◻️ A set of transport calculations  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: No quantities or calculations appear._
- ◻️ An explicit policy argument  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: No policy claim is advanced._
- ✅ Descriptive

Full working after a second miss:

> Selected sensory qualities build an impression.  
> So the answer is “Descriptive”.  

> Original excerpt: The hall should stay open later because many pupils arrive after chores. Which dominant function is supported?

- ✅ Argumentative
- ◻️ Only a neutral physical description  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: It advocates a policy with a reason._
- ◻️ Only a list with no claim  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Should and because connect claim and reason._
- ◻️ A narrative solely because pupils are mentioned  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Mentioning people does not establish an event sequence._

### `el6-5-terms` · Lesson 5 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus? | essential point | compression · qualification · source fidelity | That concerns expressing relevant information more concisely, rather than information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus. · That concerns a limitation needed for accurate meaning, rather than information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus. · That concerns keeping a summary within the source’s meaning, rather than information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus. | Selection depends on the task and main meaning. |
| Which term means expressing relevant information more concisely? | compression | essential point · qualification · source fidelity | That concerns information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus, rather than expressing relevant information more concisely. · That concerns a limitation needed for accurate meaning, rather than expressing relevant information more concisely. · That concerns keeping a summary within the source’s meaning, rather than expressing relevant information more concisely. | Shortening must preserve meaning. |
| Which term means a limitation needed for accurate meaning? | qualification | essential point · compression · source fidelity | That concerns information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus, rather than a limitation needed for accurate meaning. · That concerns expressing relevant information more concisely, rather than a limitation needed for accurate meaning. · That concerns keeping a summary within the source’s meaning, rather than a limitation needed for accurate meaning. | A contrast may carry a central limit. |
| Which term means keeping a summary within the source’s meaning? | source fidelity | essential point · compression · qualification | That concerns information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus, rather than keeping a summary within the source’s meaning. · That concerns expressing relevant information more concisely, rather than keeping a summary within the source’s meaning. · That concerns a limitation needed for accurate meaning, rather than keeping a summary within the source’s meaning. | Source information and new advice are different tasks. |

- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus, rather than keeping a summary within the source’s meaning._

### `el6-5-apply` · Lesson 5 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original source: The group labelled tools and recorded loans. Lost items were reported less often, but no independent count was made. Which summary is faithful? | Labelling and loan records coincided with fewer loss reports, without an independent count | The trial proved no tool was ever lost · Every school must use identical labels · Independent counts confirmed a permanent result | Fewer reports do not prove no losses. · That recommendation is not supplied. · The source explicitly lacks independent counts. | Preserve both reported change and evidential limit. |
| Original source: Bih offered morning and afternoon practice. Attendance was higher in the morning; reasons were not investigated. Which summary fits? | Morning practice had higher attendance, for reasons not investigated | Pupils disliked all afternoon work · Morning practice guarantees better learning · The source proves household tasks caused absence | A preference or dislike is not established. · Attendance is not a learning-outcome measure. · No cause was investigated. | Condense the comparison without inventing its cause. |
| Original source: A board displayed opening times twice in the same paragraph, then explained where to ask about changes. What can usually be compressed? | The repeated opening-time information | The only instruction for finding updates · Every fact about access · An invented penalty for lateness | That instruction may be central to access. · Compression should retain relevant meaning. · A penalty is absent from the source. | Remove repetition before essential information. |

### `el6-5-repair` · Lesson 5 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ essential point: information necessary to the source’s central meaning or task focus.
- ✅ compression: expressing relevant information more concisely.
- ✅ qualification: a limitation needed for accurate meaning.
- ✅ source fidelity: keeping a summary within the source’s meaning.
- ❌ Every example is always essential.  
  _↳ Selection depends on the task and main meaning._
- ❌ Compression permits inventing a stronger outcome.  
  _↳ Shortening must preserve meaning._
- ❌ Every but clause can be deleted without changing meaning.

| Which term means a conclusion reasonably connected to supplied clues? | supported inference | textual clue · certainty limit · alternative explanation | That concerns a detail that supports an interpretation, rather than a conclusion reasonably connected to supplied clues. · That concerns a restriction on how strongly evidence permits a claim, rather than a conclusion reasonably connected to supplied clues. · That concerns another account compatible with the evidence, rather than a conclusion reasonably connected to supplied clues. | A precaution does not prove the feared damage happened. |

### `el6-6-apply` · Lesson 6 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original passage: Ayuk checked the clouds and placed a cover over the seed trays. Which inference is supported? | He may be protecting the trays from expected weather | He certainly knows tomorrow’s rainfall total · Every tray was already destroyed · He owns every farm nearby | No exact forecast is supplied. · Precaution does not establish destruction. · Ownership beyond the trays is not stated. | Cloud checking and a cover support a weather precaution. |
| Original passage: Before speaking, Bih read her notes again and took a slow breath. | Original passage: The hall was dark when the group arrived. Nothing else is said. ### `el6-6-repair` · Lesson 6 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ textual clue: a detail that supports an interpretation.
- ✅ supported inference: a conclusion reasonably connected to supplied clues.
- ✅ certainty limit: a restriction on how strongly evidence permits a claim.
- ✅ alternative explanation: another account compatible with the evidence.
- ❌ An inference needs no evidence from the passage.  
  _↳ The action provides a basis for interpretation._
- ❌ Concern about water proves every book is already ruined.  
  _↳ A precaution does not prove the feared damage happened._
- ❌ Missing ownership information proves nobody owns it.  
  _↳ Unstated information remains unknown._
- ❌ Only one possible motive can ever fit an action.  
  _↳ Evidence can leave more than one explanation open._

| Original prose: I thought the locked door was a warning, though I had not asked the caretaker. What should a reader distinguish? | The narrator’s interpretation from an established explanation | A proven statement by the caretaker · Proof the door was never locked · The real author’s confirmed intention | The caretaker has not been asked. · The lock is part of the narrated observation. · The narrator’s belief does not establish authorial intention. | A first-person account can reveal a limited viewpoint. |

### `el6-7-repair` · Lesson 7 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ characterisation: the presentation of a fictional person through detail and behaviour.
- ✅ setting: the place, time and conditions of a story.
- ✅ narrative viewpoint: the position from which events are presented.
- ✅ interpretive evidence: a textual detail linked to a claim about meaning.
- ❌ An action proves the character acts identically forever.  
  _↳ Character interpretation must match the particular evidence._
- ❌ Setting can never affect a character’s choices.  
  _↳ Conditions can create obstacles and opportunities._
- ❌ Every narrator is identical to the real author.  
  _↳ A constructed voice and its author are distinct._
- ❌ A character label needs no quoted or described action.  
  _↳ Evidence connects a reading to the passage._

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Use the printed British-model key. Which pair contrasts /iː/ and /ɪ/? | sheep / ship | bed / cat · put / food · cup / bird | That key pair contrasts /e/ and /æ/. · That key pair contrasts /ʊ/ and /uː/. · That key pair contrasts /ʌ/ and /ɜː/. | Read the stated sound model rather than guessing from letters. |
| Use the printed key. Which word illustrates /ʊ/? | put | food · cat · sheep | Food illustrates /uː/ here. · Cat illustrates /æ/ here. · Sheep illustrates /iː/ here. | The key distinguishes put from food. |
| Use the printed key. What is the final vowel shown for sofa? | /ə/ | /æ/ · /ɪ/ · /ɔː/ | The model assigns /æ/ to cat. · The model assigns /ɪ/ to ship. · The model assigns /ɔː/ to law. | The unstressed final model vowel is schwa. |
| Use the printed key. Which word illustrates /e/? | bed | cat · bird · hot | Cat illustrates /æ/. · Bird illustrates /ɜː/. · Hot illustrates /ɒ/. | The supplied key gives bed as the /e/ model. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”

- ◻️ /æ/

  _↳ feedback if chosen: The model assigns /ɔː/ to law._
- ✅ /ə/
- ◻️ /ɪ/  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The model assigns /ɪ/ to ship._

Full working after a second miss:

> The unstressed final model vowel is schwa.  
> So the answer is “/ə/”.

- ✅ sheep / ship
- ◻️ put / food  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That key pair contrasts /ʊ/ and /uː/._
- ◻️ bed / cat  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That key pair contrasts /e/ and /æ/._
- ◻️ cup / bird  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That key pair contrasts /ʌ/ and /ɜː/._

Full working after a second miss:

> Read the stated sound model rather than guessing from letters.  
> So the answer is “sheep / ship”.  

> Use the printed key. Which word illustrates /ʊ/?

- ✅ put
- ◻️ sheep  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Sheep illustrates /iː/ here._
- ◻️ food  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Food illustrates /uː/ here._
- ◻️ cat  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Cat illustrates /æ/ here._

Full working after a second miss:

> The key distinguishes put from food.  
> So the answer is “put”.

### `el6-12-repair` · Lesson 12 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ adjective function: modification of a noun or description as a complement.
- ✅ adverb function: modification of a verb, adjective, adverb or clause.
- ✅ preposition function: heading a phrase with a complement to express a relationship.
- ✅ conjunction function: connection of words, phrases or clauses.
- ❌ Careful and carefully have identical functions in every sentence.  
  _↳ Forms must suit their role in the sentence._
- ❌ Every modifier of a verb must be an adjective.  
  _↳ An adverb can describe how an action is performed._
- ❌ Beside is the finite verb in this phrase.  
  _↳ The phrase expresses location rather than a finite action._
- ❌ Because and although always express the same relationship.  
  _↳ Reason and concession are different connections._

  _↳ explanation: This is correct: heading a phrase with a complement to express a relationship. Look for a claim that conflicts with a rule or distinction taught in Learn._
- ✅ Every modifier of a verb must be an adjective.

### `el6-13-terms` · Lesson 13 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means retaining the source’s core relationships and scope? | meaning preservation | structural change · qualification retention · acknowledgement | That concerns expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern, rather than retaining the source’s core relationships and scope. · That concerns keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage, rather than retaining the source’s core relationships and scope. · That concerns recognition of another source’s idea when required, rather than retaining the source’s core relationships and scope. | Quantity changes can distort the claim. |
| Which term means expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern? | structural change | meaning preservation · qualification retention · acknowledgement | That concerns retaining the source’s core relationships and scope, rather than expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern. · That concerns keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage, rather than expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern. · That concerns recognition of another source’s idea when required, rather than expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern. | A new structure can express the same relationship. |
| Which term means keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage? | qualification retention | meaning preservation · structural change · acknowledgement | That concerns retaining the source’s core relationships and scope, rather than keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage. · That concerns expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern, rather than keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage. · That concerns recognition of another source’s idea when required, rather than keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage. | Changing certainty changes meaning. |
| Which term means recognition of another source’s idea when required? | acknowledgement | meaning preservation · structural change · qualification retention | That concerns retaining the source’s core relationships and scope, rather than recognition of another source’s idea when required. · That concerns expressing an idea through a different sentence pattern, rather than recognition of another source’s idea when required. · That concerns keeping limits such as possibility or partial coverage, rather than recognition of another source’s idea when required. | Authorship of wording and origin of an idea differ. |

### `el6-13-apply` · Lesson 13 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paraphrase faithfully: The trial may reduce queues. | The trial could make queues shorter. | The trial certainly ended every queue · Queues caused the trial to fail · No trial was held | May does not establish certainty or universality. · The source does not state that causal reversal. · The existence of a trial is supplied. | Retain possibility and the intended effect. |
| Paraphrase faithfully: Only registered members may borrow the folders. | Borrowing the folders is restricted to registered members. | Anyone may borrow without registering · Registered members must borrow every folder · No registered member may borrow | Only restricts eligibility. · Permission does not create an obligation. · That reverses the permission. | Preserve the eligibility restriction and permission. |
| Paraphrase faithfully: Bih delayed the meeting because the room was unavailable. | The room’s unavailability caused Bih to postpone the meeting. | Bih cancelled every future meeting · The meeting made all rooms unavailable · Bih delayed it because she disliked the members | A delay is not permanent cancellation. · That reverses the stated cause. · The proposed motive is invented. | Keep actor, action and cause. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Paraphrase faithfully: The trial may reduce queues.

- ✅ The trial could make queues shorter.
- ◻️ Queues caused the trial to fail  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The source does not state that causal reversal._
- ◻️ The trial certainly ended every queue  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: May does not establish certainty or universality._
- ◻️ No trial was held  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The existence of a trial is supplied._

Full working after a second miss:

> Retain possibility and the intended effect.  
> So the answer is “The trial could make queues shorter.”.  

> Paraphrase faithfully: Only registered members may borrow the folders.

- ✅ Borrowing the folders is restricted to registered members.
- ◻️ Registered members must borrow every folder  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Permission does not create an obligation._
- ◻️ Anyone may borrow without registering  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Only restricts eligibility._
- ◻️ No registered member may borrow  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That reverses the permission._

Full working after a second miss:

> Preserve the eligibility restriction and permission.  
> So the answer is “Borrowing the folders is restricted to registered members.”.  

> Paraphrase faithfully: Only registered members may borrow the folders.

- ◻️ No registered member may borrow  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That reverses the permission._
- ◻️ Registered members must borrow every folder  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Permission does not create an obligation._
- ◻️ Anyone may borrow without registering  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Only restricts eligibility._
- ✅ Borrowing the folders is restricted to registered members.

Full working after a second miss:

> Preserve the eligibility restriction and permission.  
> So the answer is “Borrowing the folders is restricted to registered members.”.

### `el6-15-terms` · Lesson 15 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means a stable perspective through which the scene is presented? | consistent viewpoint | relevant detail · character choice · coherent consequence | That concerns a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning, rather than a stable perspective through which the scene is presented. · That concerns an action that reveals priorities under a constraint, rather than a stable perspective through which the scene is presented. · That concerns a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions, rather than a stable perspective through which the scene is presented. | A limited viewpoint need not supply information it cannot know. |
| Which term means a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning? | relevant detail | consistent viewpoint · character choice · coherent consequence | That concerns a stable perspective through which the scene is presented, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning. · That concerns an action that reveals priorities under a constraint, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning. · That concerns a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning. | Select details that help the intended effect. |
| Which term means an action that reveals priorities under a constraint? | character choice | consistent viewpoint · relevant detail · coherent consequence | That concerns a stable perspective through which the scene is presented, rather than an action that reveals priorities under a constraint. · That concerns a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning, rather than an action that reveals priorities under a constraint. · That concerns a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions, rather than an action that reveals priorities under a constraint. | Choices and actions can reveal priorities. |
| Which term means a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions? | coherent consequence | consistent viewpoint · relevant detail · character choice | That concerns a stable perspective through which the scene is presented, rather than a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions. · That concerns a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning, rather than a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions. · That concerns an action that reveals priorities under a constraint, rather than a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions. | Consequences should follow intelligibly from the established situation. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

> Which term means a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning?

- ◻️ character choice  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns an action that reveals priorities under a constraint, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning._
- ◻️ consistent viewpoint  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a stable perspective through which the scene is presented, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning._
- ◻️ coherent consequence  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That concerns a result connected to the scene’s earlier actions, rather than a selected feature contributing to mood, action or meaning._

### `el6-17-apply` · Lesson 17 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which part is a prefix in unhappy? | un- | -ness · -ful · happy | That suffix is not present. · That suffix is not present. · Happy is the base. | Un- is added before the base. |

| Original scene: [Bih reads the notice again, then hands it to Tabe.] How can this direction affect the scene? | It stages a pause and a shared look at evidence | It proves every booking issue is resolved · It removes the need for any actor · It makes the notice’s contents automatically false | Checking is not a final resolution. · The action still requires performance. · Reading and sharing do not establish falsity. | Explain the action’s effect without inventing an outcome. |

### `el6-18-repair` · Lesson 18 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect claim or language form.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ dialogue: words spoken by dramatic characters.
- ✅ stage direction: written guidance for action or presentation in performance.
- ✅ conflict: a clash of aims or pressures motivating action.
- ✅ plot development: an organised change in action or situation.
- ❌ Everything a character says is proved true by being spoken.  
  _↳ Speech can express a claim, misunderstanding or intention._
- ❌ Stage directions must always be spoken aloud by characters.  
  _↳ They guide performance rather than necessarily belonging to dialogue._
- ❌ Conflict must always involve physical violence.  
  _↳ Competing aims can drive a non-violent dramatic conflict._
- ❌ Plot is only a list of unrelated character names.  
  _↳ Action develops through connected events and choices._

> Find the incorrect claim or language form.

- ◻️ conflict: a clash of aims or pressures motivating action.

| q | r | w | e | y |

| The room was open, not ___. Choose the direct state contrast. | closed | blue · quiet · large | Colour is a different dimension. · Noise is a different dimension. · Size is a different dimension. | Antonyms contrast along the relevant meaning dimension. |
| In the supplied pronunciation model, flour and flower sound alike but have different meanings. Which relationship is shown? | Homophony | Antonymy · A prefix relationship · Identical spelling | Their meanings are not opposites. · Neither is formed by prefixing the other. · Their spellings differ. | Shared model pronunciation identifies homophones. |
| A bat flew above the pitch; a player held a bat. Under this lesson’s narrow convention, what is illustrated? | Homonymy | Antonymy · Two words with different spellings · A common suffix | Animal and implement are not opposite ends of a scale. · Both uses are spelled bat. · No suffix relationship is described. | The form is shared while the contextual meanings differ. |

- ✅ faithful retelling: a reordered or condensed account preserving the source’s meaning.
- ❌ After and before express the same order.  
  _↳ Time connectors determine different event relationships._
- ❌ Any earlier event must cause every later event.  
  _↳ Time order alone does not establish causation._

  _↳ The source’s important change helps explain what follows._
- ❌ Inventing dialogue is always permitted in a factual retelling.  
  _↳ Unstated additions must not be presented as source events._

| Choose the noun: Bih offered useful ___. | advice | advise · advisable · advising us | Advise is the verb, not the intended noun. · Advisable is adjectival. · That phrase does not fit the stated noun pattern. | Advice is the uncountable noun in this use. |

### `el6-24-apply` · Lesson 24 · multiple choice, level 2

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Choose the edited agreement: The list of requests ___ ready. | is | are · be · were being be | Requests is inside an of phrase; list is the subject head. · Be alone is not the present finite form needed. · That sequence does not form the intended predicate. | The singular head list controls is. |
| Two independent clauses are intended with no causal claim: Bih checked the note, Tabe waited. Which minimal formal repair preserves that? | Bih checked the note; Tabe waited. | Bih checked the note Tabe waited. · Because Bih checked the note. · Bih checked the note, because Tabe always lied. | Removing punctuation leaves a fused sentence. · That leaves only a dependent clause and drops the other event. · The invented cause and lying claim change the message. | A semicolon can join related independent clauses without adding a cause. |
| Bih told Ngozi that she would lead. The writer means Ngozi. Which repair makes that reference explicit? | Bih told Ngozi that Ngozi would lead. | Bih told Ngozi that Bih would lead. · Nobody would lead. · They had never spoken. | That selects the other person. · That removes the stated plan. · That contradicts the speech event. | Repeating the intended name removes this ambiguity. |

Right answer: `{X.r}`; wrong choices: `{X.w[0]}`, `{X.w[1]}`, `{X.w[2]}`

- Feedback `w0`: “{X.e[0]}”
- Feedback `w1`: “{X.e[1]}”
- Feedback `w2`: “{X.e[2]}”

Three generated variants:

> Two independent clauses are intended with no causal claim: Bih checked the note, Tabe waited. Which minimal formal repair preserves that?

- ◻️ Bih checked the note Tabe waited.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Removing punctuation leaves a fused sentence._
- ✅ Bih checked the note; Tabe waited.
- ◻️ Because Bih checked the note.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That leaves only a dependent clause and drops the other event._
- ◻️ Bih checked the note, because Tabe always lied.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The invented cause and lying claim change the message._

Full working after a second miss:

> A semicolon can join related independent clauses without adding a cause.  
> So the answer is “Bih checked the note; Tabe waited.”.  

> Choose the edited agreement: The list of requests ___ ready.

- ◻️ were being be  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That sequence does not form the intended predicate._
- ◻️ are  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Requests is inside an of phrase; list is the subject head._
- ✅ is
- ◻️ be  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Be alone is not the present finite form needed._

Full working after a second miss:

> The singular head list controls is.  
> So the answer is “is”.  

> Two independent clauses are intended with no causal claim: Bih checked the note, Tabe waited. Which minimal formal repair preserves that?

- ◻️ Because Bih checked the note.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: That leaves only a dependent clause and drops the other event._
- ✅ Bih checked the note; Tabe waited.
- ◻️ Bih checked the note Tabe waited.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: Removing punctuation leaves a fused sentence._
- ◻️ Bih checked the note, because Tabe always lied.  
  _↳ feedback if chosen: The invented cause and lying claim change the message._

Full working after a second miss:

> A semicolon can join related independent clauses without adding a cause.  
> So the answer is “Bih checked the note; Tabe waited.”.

### `el6-26-terms` · Lesson 26 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute? | controlling impression | spatial organisation · sensory detail · qualified interpretation | That concerns an order guiding attention through a described space, rather than the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute. · That concerns a specific impression associated with perception, rather than the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute. · That concerns a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests, rather than the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute. | Selection helps details work towards a coherent effect. |
| Which term means an order guiding attention through a described space? | spatial organisation | controlling impression · sensory detail · qualified interpretation | That concerns the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute, rather than an order guiding attention through a described space. · That concerns a specific impression associated with perception, rather than an order guiding attention through a described space. · That concerns a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests, rather than an order guiding attention through a described space. | A spatial path helps the reader follow attention. |
| Which term means a specific impression associated with perception? | sensory detail | controlling impression · spatial organisation · qualified interpretation | That concerns the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute, rather than a specific impression associated with perception. · That concerns an order guiding attention through a described space, rather than a specific impression associated with perception. · That concerns a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests, rather than a specific impression associated with perception. | Relevant concrete detail can convey an impression efficiently. |
| Which term means a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests? | qualified interpretation | controlling impression · spatial organisation · sensory detail | That concerns the main effect towards which descriptive details contribute, rather than a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests. · That concerns an order guiding attention through a described space, rather than a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests. · That concerns a specific impression associated with perception, rather than a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests. | The narrator’s impression should not exceed its evidence. |

### `el6-26-repair` · Lesson 26 · spot the error, level 3

- ✅ qualified interpretation: a judgement limited to what observed detail suggests.

  _↳ A spatial path helps the reader follow attention._
